<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that every permitted minimal model program for a projective log canonical fourfold with rational boundary over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero terminates. The result allows arbitrary negative extremal rays and mixed birational steps on the given models, without pseudo-effectivity or initial ℚ-factoriality.

- **claim-arbitrary-lc-program-termination** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $k$ be an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero, and let $(X,B)$ be a projective lc pair of dimension four with rational boundary. No $\mathbb Q$-factoriality or pseudo-effectivity is assumed.* 1. *Every negative extremal ray at every stage has the contraction and, when birational, a positive model required by Definition <a href="#def:program" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:program">1</a>.* 2. *Every program of Definition <a href="#def:program" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:program">1</a> starting from $(X,B)$ has finitely many birational steps, for every sequence of permitted choices.* 3. *Every maximal program ends either at a projective lc pair with nef adjoint, or at a Mori fibre space: a projective contraction with connected fibres, relative Picard number one, lower-dimensional normal base, and relatively ample negative adjoint.* *An already nef input has a zero-step completed program.
  - Explanation: Every permitted program from the given projective lc fourfold terminates, with continuation through every chosen negative ray and the stated endpoint alternatives.
- **claim-full-graph-discrepancy-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-graph)): Let $G$ be the normalization of the graph of the birational map in <a href="#eq:step-diagram" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:step-diagram">[eq:step-diagram]</a>, with projections $u:G\to X^-$ and $v:G\to X^+$. Then $$H=u^*D^- -v^*D^+\geq0, \qquad \operatorname{Supp}H=(G\to Z)^{-1}\Sigma.$$ The divisor $H$ is exceptional over $X^+$, and $-H$ is ample over $Z$. For every divisorial valuation $\xi$ of the common function field, $$\label{eq:discrepancy-comparison} a(\xi;X^+,B^+)-a(\xi;X^-,B^-)=\xi(H)\geq0.$$ The inequality is strict exactly when $c_{X^-}(\xi)\subset W^-$, equivalently when $c_{X^+}(\xi)\subset W^+$.* *For a nontrivial step, $$\label{eq:graph-dimension} \dim W^-+\dim W^+\geq3.$$ Always $\dim W^+\leq2$. If $f^-$ is also small, then $\dim W^-\leq2$ and $\dim\Sigma\leq1$.
  - Explanation: The effective graph difference detects precisely the full bad loci. Discrepancies improve there, and the dimensions of the bad loci constrain every nontrivial step.
- **claim-singularity-preservation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-singularities-preserved)): Under the hypotheses of Lemma <a href="#lem:graph" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:graph">3</a>, lc and klt are preserved. For a small step, an effective terminal pair remains terminal. At a divisor-losing step the discrepancy of every lost prime divisor increases strictly.
  - Explanation: Graph comparison preserves lc and klt singularities, preserves terminality for small steps, and strictly improves discrepancies of lost divisors.
- **claim-cycle-rank-localization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cycle-rank)): Let $V$ be projective and $A\subset V$ closed of dimension at most $r$. Restriction to $V\setminus A$ maps the span of $r$-cycle classes onto the corresponding Borel–Moore cycle span. Its kernel is exactly the span of the classes of the $r$-dimensional irreducible components of $A$. In particular,* 1. *removing a set of dimension less than $r$ does not change this rank;* 2. *removing a set with an $r$-dimensional component strictly lowers the rank.* *For a sequence of diagrams as in Lemma <a href="#lem:graph" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:graph">3</a>, only finitely many steps lose a prime divisor. On a small tail with no positive-side bad surfaces, only finitely many steps have a negative-side bad surface.
  - Explanation: Localization identifies the cycle-span kernel with the top-dimensional removed components. Its rank decreases exclude infinitely many divisor losses and, under the additional small-tail condition, infinitely many negative-side bad surfaces.
- **claim-positive-surfaces-suffice** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-surfaces-suffice)): To prove termination of a fourfold program it suffices to show that, on its small tail, positive-side bad surfaces occur only finitely often.
  - Explanation: Finiteness of positive-side bad surfaces on a small tail is sufficient for termination.
- **claim-nef-ends-identify-ample-model** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-two-ends)): Let $V_1$ and $V_2$ be normal projective birational models over a normal base $Z$, with relatively nef $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisors $D_1,D_2$. On a smooth common model $W$, with projections $q_j:W\to V_j$, suppose $$A=q_1^*D_1+F_1=q_2^*D_2+F_2,$$ where $F_j\geq0$ is exceptional over $V_j$. Then $q_1^*D_1=q_2^*D_2$. If $D_2$ is ample over $Z$, the birational map $V_1\dashrightarrow V_2$ is a morphism $h$, and $D_1=h^*D_2$.
  - Explanation: Effective exceptional comparisons force equality of nef pullbacks. An ample prescribed end is then reached by a morphism from the other end.
- **claim-snc-discrepancy-calculation** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-snc-valuations)): Let $\pi:W\to X$ be a log resolution of a klt pair, and include the exceptional locus and the crepant boundary support in a simple normal crossings (SNC) divisor $\sum_jT_j$. Write $a_j=a(T_j;X,B)>0$, assigning $a_j=1$ to any auxiliary component of coefficient zero. If $J$ indexes the components through the generic center of a divisorial valuation $\xi$ on $W$, then $$\label{eq:snc-formula} a(\xi;X,B)=\sum_{j\in J}\xi(T_j)a_j+e(\xi).$$ Here $e(\xi)$ is a nonnegative integer, the log discrepancy for the reduced SNC boundary near that center. If the center has codimension $c$ in $W$, then $$e(\xi)\geq c-\|J\|.$$ If $e(\xi)=0$, then $\xi$ is the monomial valuation associated to a primitive positive integral vector over the generic point of an SNC stratum. In particular there are finitely many such valuations when their orders $\xi(T_j)$ are bounded.
  - Explanation: The SNC discrepancy formula separates positive boundary contributions from an integral error. Zero error characterizes monomial divisorial orders and gives finiteness when the orders are bounded.
- **claim-finite-small-discrepancies** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-small-discrepancies)): For a fixed klt pair $(X,B)$ there are finitely many exceptional divisorial valuations with $a(\xi;X,B)\leq1$.
  - Explanation: A fixed klt pair has only finitely many exceptional divisorial valuations at or below the stated discrepancy threshold.
- **claim-echo-classification** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-echoes)): Let $(Y,\Theta)$ be an effective terminal pair on a projective $\mathbb Q$-factorial fourfold. Label finitely many prime divisors $S_h$ containing $\operatorname{Supp}\Theta$, and write $\Theta=\sum_hb_hS_h$, allowing $b_h=0$. Then:* 1. *Only finitely many exceptional valuations with $a(\xi;Y,\Theta)<2$ have center of codimension at least three.* 2. *At each fixed surface center there are only finitely many exceptional valuations with log discrepancy less than two.* 3. *Apart from finitely many surface centers, every valuation with surface center and log discrepancy less than two is centered on the one-label smooth open. In regular parameters $x,t$ at its generic point, with $x=0$ defining that label $S_h$, the contributing valuations are exactly the monomial orders $$\label{eq:echo-values} \xi(x)=j,\quad \xi(t)=1,\qquad a(\xi;Y,\Theta)=1+j(1-b_h)<2,\qquad j\geq1.$$* *More strongly, all exceptional valuations below two which are not of the form <a href="#eq:echo-values" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:echo-values">[eq:echo-values]</a> on that one-label smooth open belong to a finite list.
  - Explanation: Exceptional valuations below the discrepancy threshold form ordinary monomial echo families on the smooth single-label locus, with only finitely many exceptions.
- **claim-crepant-terminal-bridges** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-terminal-chain)): There are projective birational morphisms $p_i:Y_i\to X_i$ extracting exactly $I_0$, with $Y_i$ $\mathbb Q$-factorial and $$\label{eq:terminal-crepant} K_{Y_i}+\Theta_i=p_i^*(K_{X_i}+B_i),\qquad \Theta_i=B_i^{\mathrm{str}}+ \sum_{s\in I_0}(1-a_i(s))E_{i,s},$$ such that each $(Y_i,\Theta_i)$ is effective terminal. Consecutive junctions can be joined by:* 1. *decreasing the coefficient of each $E_{i,s}$ to $1-a_{i+1}(s)$ on $Y_i$;* 2. *a finite sequence, possibly empty, of small negative terminal flips over $Z_i$.* *Every labeled prime persists through the chain. All coefficients are nonincreasing, and the discrepancy of every valuation is nondecreasing, including across coefficient changes.
  - Explanation: A stabilized small klt tail lifts to effective terminal junctions joined by coefficient decreases and finite small-flip bridges, retaining the prescribed downstairs models.
- **claim-transverse-surface-mld** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-transverse-mld)): The numbers $\operatorname{mld}_{\eta_V}(X_i,B_i)$, as $i$ and $V$ vary, belong to the set of surface mld values with the same finite set of boundary coefficients. Each minimum is attained.
  - Explanation: Generic surface-center minimal log discrepancies occur among surface-pair values with the original finite coefficient set, and the infima are attained.
- **claim-varying-labels-have-small-image** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-codim-two)): After truncating the small downstairs tail, every surface contained in either bad locus of every remaining step has generic-point mld greater than one and is generically in the smooth locus of its ambient model. At each remaining crepant junction, every varying label has downstairs center of codimension at least three. Consequently the reduced union of varying labels is mapped by $p_i$ to a set of dimension at most one.
  - Explanation: Surface mld ACC removes bad surfaces with small discrepancy and forces varying extracted labels to have downstairs images of dimension at most one.
- **claim-default-weights-stabilize** (theorem, [source](#stmt-difficulty-preparation)): Each $d_h$ is a finite nonnegative integer and is nonincreasing along the terminal chain. After discarding finitely many junctions, all $d_h$ are constant, including throughout every intervening finite bridge.
  - Explanation: Default echo weights are finite nonnegative integers. Their monotonicity and the finite label set permit a tail on which all defaults are constant.
- **claim-local-ceiling-deficit** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-ceiling-error)): Let $V\subset Y$ be an irreducible surface on a terminal model in the prepared chain, and set $$r_V^{\mathrm{var}}=\sum_{h\text{ varying}}r_{V,h}.$$ Then $$\label{eq:local-ceiling-bound} \sum_{c_Y(v)=V}w_v-\sum_h r_{V,h}d_h \ \geq\ -\max\{r_V^{\mathrm{var}}-1,0\}.$$ The assertion does not require the boundary to have simple normal crossings at $\eta_V$.
  - Explanation: At each surface, varying normalization branches account for the possible negative correction. Fixed coefficients introduce no rounding deficit, and SNC is unnecessary.
- **claim-global-branch-inequality** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-branch)): Let $p\colon Y\to X$ be a projective birational morphism of normal projective fourfolds over $\mathbb C$. Suppose that $Y$ is $\mathbb Q$-factorial and terminal and that $(Y,\Theta)$ is an effective terminal pair with rational boundary. Let $$Q=\bigcup_{j=1}^{m}Q_j$$ be a reduced union of distinct positive-coefficient components of $\Theta$, and assume $\dim p(Q)\leq 1$. Write $Q_j^\nu$ for the normalization of $Q_j$. For each irreducible surface $V\subset Q$, let $r_V$ be the number of prime divisors of $\coprod_jQ_j^\nu$ mapping onto $V$. Then $$\label{eq:branch-inequality} \begin{aligned} \sum_{V\subset Q}(r_V-1) &\leq\sum_{j=1}^{m}\rho_*(Q_j^\nu)\\ &\quad+\#\bigl\{v:\operatorname{codim}_Yc_Y(v)=3,\; c_Y(v)\subset Q,\;a(v;Y,\Theta)<2\bigr\}. \end{aligned}$$ Here the valuations are normalized divisorial orders, and the sum on the left has only finitely many nonzero terms.
  - Explanation: Total excess algebraic normalization branches is bounded by normalization divisor-cycle ranks and distinct curve-centered valuations whose boundary discrepancies lie below two.
- **claim-conductor-cohomology-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-conductor)): With the notation above, $$\label{eq:conductor-rank} \sum_{V\subset Q}(r_V-1)-\sum_j\rho_*(Q_j^\nu) \leq \operatorname{rank}\operatorname{Gr}^{W}_{4} \bigl[H^5(P)\longrightarrow H^5(Q)\bigr].$$
  - Explanation: Normalization excision bounds branch excess beyond divisor-cycle ranks by the weight-four rank of the cohomology restriction map.
- **claim-restricted-intersection-complex-purity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-restriction-purity)): Let $p\colon Y\to X$ be a projective birational morphism of normal projective fourfolds over $\mathbb C$, and let $C\subset X$ be a closed reduced subset of dimension at most one. Put $P=(p^{-1}C)_{\mathrm{red}}$. Then $$\mathbb H^1(P,\mathrm{IC}_Y\|_P)$$ is pure of weight five.
  - Explanation: Projective decomposition and strict support estimates make the indicated restricted intersection-complex hypercohomology pure.
- **claim-singular-curve-local-system-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-local-system-bound)): For $P$ and $Q$ in <a href="#eq:branch-loci" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:branch-loci">[eq:branch-loci]</a>, $$\label{eq:local-system-rank} \operatorname{rank}\operatorname{Gr}^{W}_{4} \bigl[H^5(P)\longrightarrow H^5(Q)\bigr] \leq \sum_{\substack{T\text{ irreducible curve}\\ T\subset\operatorname{Sing}Y\cap Q}}b_T.$$
  - Explanation: The comparison cone confines the relevant cohomology rank to coinvariant contributions from singular curves contained in the boundary union.
- **claim-local-cohomology-produces-valuations** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-local-valuations)): Let $Y$ be a normal projective $\mathbb Q$-factorial fourfold, and let $(Y,\Theta)$ be an effective terminal pair with rational boundary. Let $T$ be an irreducible curve in $\operatorname{Sing}Y$ contained in a positive-coefficient component of $\Theta$. Put $$j:Y_{\mathrm{reg}}\hookrightarrow Y, \qquad \mathcal V_T=(R^2j_*\mathbb Q)\|_{T^\circ}, \qquad b_T=\dim_{\mathbb Q}H_c^2(T^\circ,\mathcal V_T),$$ where $T^\circ$ is a sufficiently small smooth dense open on which the indicated sheaf is a local system. There are at least $b_T$ distinct normalized divisorial valuations $v$ of $\mathbb C(Y)$ such that $$c_Y(v)=T,\qquad a(v;Y,0)=2,\qquad a(v;Y,\Theta)<2.$$ The valuations obtained for different curves $T$ are distinct.
  - Explanation: Each eligible singular curve supplies enough distinct normalized divisorial valuations to pay for its local-system contribution. Positive boundary lowers their zero-boundary discrepancies strictly.
- **claim-local-system-split-surjection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-local-split-punctured)): Restriction to the smooth open defines a split surjection of rational local systems $$r:\mathcal W\longrightarrow\mathcal V_T.$$ The splitting is available on $T^\circ$ itself, before passing to any cover.
  - Explanation: The resolution-fibre local system maps onto punctured cohomology with a splitting already on the original smooth curve open.
- **claim-scheme-fibre-coherent-vanishing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-local-scheme-fibre-vanishing)): Let $y\in Y$ be a point for which the scheme fibre $F_y=\widetilde Y\times_Y\operatorname{Spec}\kappa(y)$ has dimension at most two. Then $$H^2(F_y,\mathcal O_{F_y})=0.$$ If $y$ is a closed complex point, first Chern classes of algebraic line bundles span $H^2(F_y,\mathbb Q)$.
  - Explanation: Rational singularities and formal functions give coherent cohomology vanishing on the actual scheme fibre. Over a closed complex point, line-bundle Chern classes consequently span rational cohomology.
- **claim-finite-monodromy-generic-line-bundles** (construction, [source](#stmt-local-generic-lines)): The local systems $\mathcal W$ and $\mathcal V_T$ have finite monodromy. Put $K_T=\kappa(\eta_T)$ and $F_{\eta_T}=\widetilde Y\times_Y\operatorname{Spec}K_T$. There are $b_T$ line bundles $$L_1,\ldots,L_{b_T}\in\operatorname{Pic}(F_{\eta_T})$$ whose spreads over a sufficiently small $T^\circ$ have linearly independent images under $r$ in every fibre of $\mathcal V_T$.
  - Explanation: Finite monodromy and norms produce line bundles over the curve's own function field with independent images in punctured cohomology.
- **claim-etale-extension-preserves-residue-field** (construction, [source](#stmt-local-etale-lines)): Let $L_1,\ldots,L_b$ be finitely many line bundles on $F_{\eta_T}$. There exist a normal integral quasi-projective variety $Y_e$, an étale morphism $q:Y_e\to Y$, and a point $\eta'\in Y_e$ above $\eta_T$ such that $$K_T=\kappa(\eta_T)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\kappa(\eta')$$ is the residue-field map, and every $L_i$ extends to a line bundle $\widetilde L_i$ on $\widetilde Y_e=\widetilde Y\times_Y Y_e$. The extensions restrict to the specified $L_i$ on the same scheme fibre. The variety $\widetilde Y_e$ is smooth and integral, and $\widetilde Y_e\to Y_e$ is projective and birational.
  - Explanation: Formal lifting, algebraization, and approximation extend the specified generic-fibre bundles to an ordinary pointed étale neighbourhood while preserving the residue field and the scheme-fibre restrictions.
- **claim-fibre-degree-vector-independence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-local-degree-independence)): The vectors $$\bigl(D_i'\cdot C_1,\ldots,D_i'\cdot C_s\bigr) \in\mathbb Q^s,\qquad 1\leq i\leq b,$$ are linearly independent. In particular $s\geq b$.
  - Explanation: Independent punctured classes yield independent degree vectors on arithmetic fibre-curve components of a small model, forcing sufficiently many such components.
- **claim-etale-valuation-restriction-injective** (theorem, [source](#stmt-local-restriction-injective)): Let $q:Y_e\to Y$ be a dominant étale morphism of normal integral complex varieties, let $\eta\in Y$, and fix $\eta'\in Y_e$ above $\eta$ such that $\kappa(\eta)\to\kappa(\eta')$ is an isomorphism. Suppose that $Y$ is $\mathbb Q$-Gorenstein. On normalized divisorial valuations of $\mathbb C(Y_e)$ with centre the closure of $\eta'$, restriction to $\mathbb C(Y)$ is already normalized, remains divisorial, and is injective. Moreover $$a(w;Y_e,0)=a(w\|_{\mathbb C(Y)};Y,0).$$
  - Explanation: At the specified residue-preserving étale point, divisorial valuation restriction is injective, retains normalization, and preserves zero-boundary discrepancy.
- **claim-contracted-divisor-cycle-independence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-difficulty-normalization-independence)): Let $q:S\to T$ be a projective birational morphism of normal projective threefolds. The cycle classes in $H_4(S,\mathbb Q)$ of the prime divisors contracted by $q$ are linearly independent.
  - Explanation: Contracted prime divisors on a normal projective threefold have independent rational homology classes, allowing exact normalization-rank comparisons.
- **claim-corrected-difficulty-descent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-difficulty)): On the prepared terminal chain, the integer $\mathcal D(Y,\Theta)$ has the following properties.* 1. *It is nonnegative at every crepant junction.* 2. *It is nonincreasing under every coefficient decrease and every small terminal flip.* 3. *If the weight of one exceptional valuation strictly decreases in one of these moves, then the difficulty strictly decreases.* *No separate nonnegativity assertion is required for the intermediate models in a bridge.
  - Explanation: The corrected difficulty is nonnegative at crepant junctions, never increases along prepared chain moves, and decreases strictly whenever an exceptional valuation loses weight.
- **claim-complex-klt-program-termination** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-klt-termination)): Every program in Definition <a href="#def:program" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:program">1</a> starting with a projective klt fourfold pair over $\mathbb C$ with rational boundary has finitely many birational steps. No pseudo-effectivity hypothesis is needed.
  - Explanation: Every permitted complex projective klt fourfold program with rational boundary has finitely many birational steps, without pseudo-effectivity.
- **claim-elementary-dlt-operations** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-dlt-elementary)): Let $(U,\Delta)$ be a projective $\mathbb Q$-factorial dlt pair with rational boundary, and let $R$ be a $(K_U+\Delta)$-negative extremal ray. Its elementary contraction exists. If it is birational and small, its flip exists, is projective, and is a $(K_U+\Delta)$-flip. Divisorial contractions and flips preserve $\mathbb Q$-factoriality and the dlt property.
  - Explanation: Negative-ray contractions and small flips exist for the stated dlt inputs and preserve the dlt and factorial properties.
- **claim-elementary-dlt-program-termination** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-dlt-termination)): Every program of elementary negative birational steps starting from a projective $\mathbb Q$-factorial dlt fourfold pair with rational boundary has finitely many steps, independently of its successive ray choices.
  - Explanation: Elementary negative birational programs from the stated dlt fourfolds terminate for arbitrary successive ray choices.
- **claim-negative-absolute-vertical-ray** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-vertical-ray)): Let $(U,\Delta)$ be a projective lc pair, and let $h:U\to Z$ be a projective morphism to a projective variety. If $K_U+\Delta$ is not nef over $Z$, there is a $(K_U+\Delta)$-negative extremal ray of the absolute cone $\overline{\mathrm{NE}}(U)$ which is contracted by $h$. Its contraction factors $h$. If $h$ is birational, that contraction is birational.
  - Explanation: Failure of relative nefness gives a negative absolute extremal ray inside the vertical face. Its contraction factors the structural morphism and is birational when that morphism is birational.
- **claim-prescribed-complex-lc-program-termination** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-lc-lift)): Every program in Definition <a href="#def:program" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:program">1</a> starting from a projective lc fourfold pair over $\mathbb C$ with rational boundary has finitely many birational steps.
  - Explanation: Nonempty finite dlt bridges follow every prescribed lc small step; concatenation excludes an infinite complex lc program.
- **claim-continuation-through-chosen-ray** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-continuation)): Let $(X,B)$ be a projective lc fourfold pair over $\mathbb C$ with rational boundary, with $D=K_X+B$ $\mathbb Q$-Cartier. Every $D$-negative extremal ray has a projective contraction with normal projective target, connected fibres, relative Picard number one, and relatively ample $-D$. If this contraction is birational, it has an output in the precise convention of Definition <a href="#def:program" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:program">1</a>: the positive map is small, possibly an isomorphism, and its actual adjoint with the strict boundary is relatively ample and lc. If it is of fibre type, it is a Mori fibre-space endpoint in that convention.
  - Explanation: Every chosen negative ray has the required contraction. Birational contractions admit a small positive model with the actual strict-boundary adjoint, including mixed steps.
- **claim-numerical-spaces-under-field-extension** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-numerical-extension)): Let $k_0\subset K$ be algebraically closed fields of characteristic zero and let $X_0$ be a projective variety over $k_0$. Base extension identifies their real numerical divisor and curve spaces and their closed effective curve cones: $$N^1(X_0)_{\mathbb R}\simeq N^1(X_{0,K})_{\mathbb R}, \qquad N_1(X_0)_{\mathbb R}\simeq N_1(X_{0,K})_{\mathbb R}, \qquad \overline{\mathrm{NE}}(X_0) =\overline{\mathrm{NE}}(X_{0,K}).$$ In fact every numerical class of an integral curve on the extension is represented by an integral curve over $k_0$. For a projective morphism between projective varieties, these identifications commute with pullback of numerical divisor classes and preserve the relative Picard number.
  - Explanation: Algebraically closed base extension preserves numerical divisor and curve spaces, effective curve cones, integral curve representatives, and relative Picard numbers.
- **claim-entire-countable-program-field-transfer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-field-transfer)): Let $k$ be any algebraically closed field of characteristic zero. A countable program of the kind in Definition <a href="#def:program" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:program">1</a>, including all its prescribed ray choices, descends to a common countable algebraically closed subfield of $k$. After embedding that subfield in $\mathbb C$, it gives a program with the same step types and relative signs, the same boundary coefficients, and the same singularity properties. In particular an infinite birational program over $k$ would give an infinite birational program over $\mathbb C$.
  - Explanation: An entire countable prescribed program transfers to the complex numbers while retaining its choices and defining properties, so an infinite original program would remain infinite.
- **claim-finite-continuation-field-transfer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-existence-transfer)): Proposition <a href="#prop:continuation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:continuation">36</a> holds over any algebraically closed characteristic-zero field, with exactly the contraction and mixed-step conventions stated there.
  - Explanation: Finite continuation diagrams spread and specialize to every stated ground field without changing the selected ray or the mixed-step convention.
- **claim-pseudo-effective-semiample-endpoints** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-cor-semiample-endpoints)): Let $k$ be an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero, and let $(X,B)$ be a projective lc fourfold pair over $k$ with rational boundary. Suppose that $K_X+B$ is pseudo-effective. Then every maximal program of Definition <a href="#def:program" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:program">1</a> ends at a projective lc pair $(Y,B_Y)$ for which some positive Cartier multiple of $K_Y+B_Y$ is generated by global sections.
  - Explanation: With log abundance, every maximal program from the stated pseudo-effective lc input ends at a semiample adjoint.
- **claim-uniform-local-mori-complements** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-cor-uniform-mori-complements)): Fix a real number $\epsilon>0$ and a finite set $I\subset[0,1]\cap\mathbb Q$. There is a positive integer $M=M(4,\epsilon,I)$ with the following property. Let $(X,B)$ be a projective $\epsilon$-lc fourfold pair over $\mathbb C$ with coefficients in $I$ and with $K_X+B$ not pseudo-effective. Every maximal program of Definition <a href="#def:program" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:program">1</a> ends at a Mori contraction $h:(Y,B_Y)\to S$. For every closed $s\in S$, there is an effective $\mathbb Q$-divisor $B_Y^+\geq B_Y$ such that, on the inverse image of some neighbourhood of $s$, the pair $(Y,B_Y^+)$ is klt and $M(K_Y+B_Y^+)$ is Cartier and linearly trivial.
  - Explanation: For the stated non-pseudo-effective uniformly lc complex inputs, every maximal program reaches a Mori endpoint admitting local klt complements with an index independent of the program.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-cycle-rank",
        "exact_quote": "Let $V$ be projective and $A\\subset V$ closed of dimension at most $r$. Restriction to $V\\setminus A$ maps the span of $r$-cycle classes onto the corresponding Borel–Moore cycle span. Its kernel is exactly the span of the classes of the $r$-dimensional irreducible components of $A$. In particular,*\n\n1.  *removing a set of dimension less than $r$ does not change this rank;*\n\n2.  *removing a set with an $r$-dimensional component strictly lowers the rank.*\n\n*For a sequence of diagrams as in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:graph\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:graph\">3</a>, only finitely many steps lose a prime divisor. On a small tail with no positive-side bad surfaces, only finitely many steps have a negative-side bad surface.",
        "source_id": "PM-056.3"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-two-ends",
        "exact_quote": "Let $V_1$ and $V_2$ be normal projective birational models over a normal base $Z$, with relatively nef $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisors $D_1,D_2$. On a smooth common model $W$, with projections $q_j:W\\to V_j$, suppose $$A=q_1^*D_1+F_1=q_2^*D_2+F_2,$$ where $F_j\\geq0$ is exceptional over $V_j$. Then $q_1^*D_1=q_2^*D_2$. If $D_2$ is ample over $Z$, the birational map $V_1\\dashrightarrow V_2$ is a morphism $h$, and $D_1=h^*D_2$.",
        "source_id": "PM-056.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-two-ends",
          "exact_quote": "Let $V_1$ and $V_2$ be normal projective birational models over a normal base $Z$, with relatively nef $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisors $D_1,D_2$. If $D_2$ is ample over $Z$, the birational map $V_1\\dashrightarrow V_2$ is a morphism $h$, and $D_1=h^*D_2$.",
          "source_id": "PM-056.3"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-snc-valuations",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\pi:W\\to X$ be a log resolution of a klt pair, and include the exceptional locus and the crepant boundary support in a simple normal crossings (SNC) divisor $\\sum_jT_j$. Write $a_j=a(T_j;X,B)>0$, assigning $a_j=1$ to any auxiliary component of coefficient zero. If $J$ indexes the components through the generic center of a divisorial valuation $\\xi$ on $W$, then $$\\label{eq:snc-formula}\n a(\\xi;X,B)=\\sum_{j\\in J}\\xi(T_j)a_j+e(\\xi).$$ Here $e(\\xi)$ is a nonnegative integer, the log discrepancy for the reduced SNC boundary near that center. If the center has codimension $c$ in $W$, then $$e(\\xi)\\geq c-|J|.$$ If $e(\\xi)=0$, then $\\xi$ is the monomial valuation associated to a primitive positive integral vector over the generic point of an SNC stratum. In particular there are finitely many such valuations when their orders $\\xi(T_j)$ are bounded.",
        "source_id": "PM-056.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-snc-valuations",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\pi:W\\to X$ be a log resolution of a klt pair, and include the exceptional locus and the crepant boundary support in a simple normal crossings (SNC) divisor $\\sum_jT_j$. Write $a_j=a(T_j;X,B)>0$, assigning $a_j=1$ to any auxiliary component of coefficient zero. If $J$ indexes the components through the generic center of a divisorial valuation $\\xi$ on $W$, then $$\\label{eq:snc-formula}\n a(\\xi;X,B)=\\sum_{j\\in J}\\xi(T_j)a_j+e(\\xi).$$ Here $e(\\xi)$ is a nonnegative integer, the log discrepancy for the reduced SNC boundary near that center. If the center has codimension $c$ in $W$, then $$e(\\xi)\\geq c-|J|.$$ If $e(\\xi)=0$, then $\\xi$ is the monomial valuation associated to a primitive positive integral vector over the generic point of an SNC stratum. In particular there are finitely many such valuations when their orders $\\xi(T_j)$ are bounded.",
          "source_id": "PM-056.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $\\pi:W\\to X$ be a log resolution of a klt pair, and include the exceptional locus and the crepant boundary support in a simple normal crossings (SNC) divisor $\\sum_jT_j$. If $J$ indexes the components through the generic center of a divisorial valuation $\\xi$ on $W$, then $$\\label{eq:snc-formula}\n a(\\xi;X,B)=\\sum_{j\\in J}\\xi(T_j)a_j+e(\\xi).$$ Here $e(\\xi)$ is a nonnegative integer, the log discrepancy for the reduced SNC boundary near that center. In particular there are finitely many such valuations when their orders $\\xi(T_j)$ are bounded.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-echoes",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(Y,\\Theta)$ be an effective terminal pair on a projective $\\mathbb Q$-factorial fourfold. Label finitely many prime divisors $S_h$ containing $\\operatorname{Supp}\\Theta$, and write $\\Theta=\\sum_hb_hS_h$, allowing $b_h=0$. Then:*\n\n1.  *Only finitely many exceptional valuations with $a(\\xi;Y,\\Theta)<2$ have center of codimension at least three.*\n\n2.  *At each fixed surface center there are only finitely many exceptional valuations with log discrepancy less than two.*\n\n\n3.  *Apart from finitely many surface centers, every valuation with surface center and log discrepancy less than two is centered on the one-label smooth open. In regular parameters $x,t$ at its generic point, with $x=0$ defining that label $S_h$, the contributing valuations are exactly the monomial orders $$\\label{eq:echo-values}\n     \\xi(x)=j,\\quad \\xi(t)=1,\\qquad\n     a(\\xi;Y,\\Theta)=1+j(1-b_h)<2,\\qquad j\\geq1.$$*\n\n*More strongly, all exceptional valuations below two which are not of the form <a href=\"#eq:echo-values\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:echo-values\">[eq:echo-values]</a> on that one-label smooth open belong to a finite list.",
        "source_id": "PM-056.3"
      },

        "exact_quote": "There are projective birational morphisms $p_i:Y_i\\to X_i$ extracting exactly $I_0$, with $Y_i$ $\\mathbb Q$-factorial and $$\\label{eq:terminal-crepant}\n K_{Y_i}+\\Theta_i=p_i^*(K_{X_i}+B_i),\\qquad\n \\Theta_i=B_i^{\\mathrm{str}}+\n       \\sum_{s\\in I_0}(1-a_i(s))E_{i,s},$$ such that each $(Y_i,\\Theta_i)$ is effective terminal.         "anchor": "stmt-thm-branch",
        "exact_quote": "Let $p\\colon Y\\to X$ be a projective birational morphism of normal projective fourfolds over $\\mathbb C$. Suppose that $Y$ is $\\mathbb Q$-factorial and terminal and that $(Y,\\Theta)$ is an effective terminal pair with rational boundary. For each irreducible surface $V\\subset Q$, let $r_V$ be the number of prime divisors of $\\coprod_jQ_j^\\nu$ mapping onto $V$. Then $$\\label{eq:branch-inequality}\n \\begin{aligned}\n \\sum_{V\\subset Q}(r_V-1)\n &\\leq\\sum_{j=1}^{m}\\rho_*(Q_j^\\nu)\\\\\n &\\quad+\\#\\bigl\\{v:\\operatorname{codim}_Yc_Y(v)=3,\\;\n          c_Y(v)\\subset Q,\\;a(v;Y,\\Theta)<2\\bigr\\}.\n \\end{aligned}$$ Here the valuations are normalized divisorial orders, and the sum on the left has only finitely many nonzero terms.",

      },

For each irreducible surface $V\\subset Q$, let $r_V$ be the number of prime divisors of $\\coprod_jQ_j^\\nu$ mapping onto $V$.         "anchor": "stmt-prop-local-valuations",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Y$ be a normal projective $\\mathbb Q$-factorial fourfold, and let $(Y,\\Theta)$ be an effective terminal pair with rational boundary. Let $T$ be an irreducible curve in $\\operatorname{Sing}Y$ contained in a positive-coefficient component of $\\Theta$. Put $$j:Y_{\\mathrm{reg}}\\hookrightarrow Y,\n \\qquad\n \\mathcal V_T=(R^2j_*\\mathbb Q)|_{T^\\circ},\n \\qquad\n b_T=\\dim_{\\mathbb Q}H_c^2(T^\\circ,\\mathcal V_T),$$ where $T^\\circ$ is a sufficiently small smooth dense open on which the indicated sheaf is a local system. There are at least $b_T$ distinct normalized divisorial valuations $v$ of $\\mathbb C(Y)$ such that $$c_Y(v)=T,\\qquad a(v;Y,0)=2,\\qquad a(v;Y,\\Theta)<2.$$ The valuations obtained for different curves $T$ are distinct.",
        "source_id": "PM-056.3"

There are at least $b_T$ distinct normalized divisorial valuations $v$ of $\\mathbb C(Y)$ such that $$c_Y(v)=T,\\qquad a(v;Y,0)=2,\\qquad a(v;Y,\\Theta)<2.$$ The valuations obtained for different curves $T$ are distinct.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-continuation",

<!-- PM:END pm.claims.v2 -->

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Permitted programs and mixed steps** ([source](#stmt-def-program)): Let $(X,B)$ be a projective lc pair. Set $(X_0,B_0)=(X,B)$ and $D_i=K_{X_i}+B_i$. At a non-nef stage choose any $D_i$-negative extremal ray of $\overline{\operatorname{NE}}(X_i)$ and its projective contraction $$f_i:X_i\longrightarrow Z_i, \qquad (f_i)_*\mathcal O_{X_i}=\mathcal O_{Z_i}, \qquad \rho(X_i/Z_i)=1.$$ The target is normal, and $-D_i$ is relatively ample. Stop if $f_i$ is of fibre type. If it is birational, a permitted step is $$X_i\xrightarrow{\ f_i\ }Z_i \xleftarrow{\ f_i^+\ }X_{i+1},$$ where $f_i^+$ is small and projective birational, possibly an isomorphism, $(X_{i+1},B_{i+1})$ is lc, $B_{i+1}$ is the strict transform of $(f_i)_*B_i$, and $D_{i+1}$ is $\mathbb Q$-Cartier and $f_i^+$-ample. There is no condition on $\rho(X_{i+1}/Z_i)$. This includes divisorial contractions, flips, and a divisorial contraction followed by a small modification. We call the last possibility a *mixed step*. Stop at a nef adjoint as well. Flops, extra blowups, and inserted isomorphisms are not steps. A program is maximal if it is continued whenever neither stopping condition holds. The broader face-contracting convention in (Han, Liu, and Zhuang 2025, Definition 2.5) [[HanLiuZhuang2025](#ref-HanLiuZhuang2025)] also allows such birational diagrams; here the negative contraction is required to contract one extremal ray.
- **Log discrepancy** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For a normalized divisorial valuation $v=\operatorname{ord}_E$, with $E$ on a smooth model $p:W\to X$, our convention is $$a(v;X,B)=1+\operatorname{coeff}_E\bigl(K_W-p^*(K_X+B)\bigr).$$
- **Exceptional divisorial valuation** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A valuation is exceptional over $X$ when this center has codimension at least two.
- **Lc and klt singularities** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Log canonical (lc) and klt mean, respectively, that every log discrepancy is nonnegative and positive.
- **Terminal pair** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A terminal pair is klt and has $a(v;X,B)>1$ for every exceptional valuation.
- **Full bad loci** ([source](#pm-h-6)): Let $U\subset Z$ be the largest open over which both morphisms are isomorphisms, and put $$\label{eq:bad-loci} \Sigma=Z\setminus U,\qquad W^-=(f^-)^{-1}\Sigma,\qquad W^+=(f^+)^{-1}\Sigma,$$ with reduced structures.
- **Algebraic cycle-span rank** ([source](#pm-h-7)): For a projective variety $V$ define $$\rho_r(V)= \dim_{\mathbb Q} \operatorname{span}\{[A]\in H_{2r}(V,\mathbb Q): A\subset V\text{ irreducible},\ \dim A=r\}.$$
- **Divisor cycle rank on a normal threefold** ([source](#pm-h-7)): On a normal projective threefold $S$ we write $\rho_*(S)=\rho_2(S)$.
- **Fixed and varying labels** ([source](#pm-h-11)): Call a label *fixed* if its coefficient is eventually constant, and *varying* otherwise.
- **Generic surface minimal log discrepancy** ([source](#pm-h-12)): For a codimension-two subvariety $V\subset X_i$, write $$\operatorname{mld}_{\eta_V}(X_i,B_i) =\inf\{a_i(\xi):c_{X_i}(\xi)=V\}.$$
- **Ceiling weight and default echo weight** ([source](#eq:difficulty-weights)): Define $$\label{eq:difficulty-weights} w(u)=\left\lceil N\max\{u,0\}\right\rceil, \qquad w_v(Y,\Theta)=w\bigl(2-a(v;Y,\Theta)\bigr), \qquad d_h=\sum_{j\geq 1}w\bigl(1-j(1-b_h)\bigr).$$
- **Algebraic normalization branch count** ([source](#pm-h-14)): For an irreducible surface $V\subset Y$, let $r_{V,h}$ be the number of prime divisors of $S_h^\nu$ mapping onto $V$, with $r_{V,h}=0$ when $V\not\subset S_h$.
- **Corrected difficulty** ([source](#stmt-def-difficulty)): For a terminal pair $(Y,\Theta)$ on the prepared chain, define $$\begin{aligned} \label{eq:difficulty} \mathcal D(Y,\Theta) ={}&\sum_{\operatorname{codim}_Y c_Y(v)\geq3}w_v \\ &+\sum_{\substack{V\subset Y\text{ irreducible}\\\dim V=2}} \left( \sum_{c_Y(v)=V}w_v-\sum_h r_{V,h}d_h \right) +\sum_h d_h\rho_*(S_h^\nu). \notag \end{aligned}$$ The correction in parentheses is performed at each individual surface before summing over surfaces.
- **Punctured curve local system and coinvariant dimension** ([source](#pm-h-20)): Define $$\label{eq:curve-local-system} \mathcal V_T=(R^2j_*\mathbb Q_{Y_{\mathrm{reg}}})\|_{T^\circ}, \qquad b_T=\dim H_c^2(T^\circ,\mathcal V_T).$$
- **Fano type contraction** ([source](#sec:endpoints)): A projective contraction $h:Y\to S$ is *of Fano type* if some effective rational boundary $\Gamma$ on $Y$ makes $(Y,\Gamma)$ klt and $-(K_Y+\Gamma)$ ample over $S$.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-negative-contraction-picard-number | \rho(X_i/Z_i) | = | $1$ | Every chosen negative extremal ray; no corresponding restriction is imposed on the positive relative Picard number. |
| q-full-bad-locus-dimension-sum | \dim W^-+\dim W^+ | ≥ | $3$ | Each nontrivial permitted birational step. |
| q-positive-bad-locus-dimension | \dim W^+ | ≤ | $2$ | All diagrams satisfying the graph-comparison hypotheses. |
| q-small-base-bad-locus-dimension | \dim\Sigma | ≤ | $1$ | The full bad locus on the common base. |
| q-snc-error-lower-bound | e(\xi) | ≥ | $c-\|J\|$ | Divisorial valuations and their generic centers on the resolution. |
| q-ordinary-echo-discrepancy | a(\xi;Y,\Theta) | = | $1+j(1-b_h)$ | Exactly the ordinary echo orders satisfying the strict discrepancy threshold. |
| q-local-branch-ceiling-deficit | \sum_{c_Y(v)=V}w_v-\sum_h r_{V,h}d_h | ≥ | $-\max\{r_V^{\mathrm{var}}-1,0\}$ | Every irreducible surface, without an SNC assumption at its generic point. |
| q-branch-multiplicity-bound | r_{V,h} | ≤ | $m_h$ | Algebraic normalization branches, including their positive residue degrees in the multiplicity calculation. |
| q-higher-echo-positive-coefficient | b_h | > | $1/2$ | A necessary condition for the specified higher echo contribution. |
| q-global-normalization-branch-budget | \sum_{V\subset Q}(r_V-1) | ≤ | $\sum_{j=1}^{m}\rho_*(Q_j^\nu)\\ &\quad+\#\bigl\{v:\operatorname{codim}_Yc_Y(v)=3,\; c_Y(v)\subset Q,\;a(v;Y,\Theta)<2\bigr\}.$ | A reduced union of positive-coefficient components mapped to a curve or a point. |
| q-proper-support-shift-bound | i+z | ≤ | $2$ | The summand shift and the dimension of its strict support. |
| q-local-valuations-zero-boundary-discrepancy | a(v;Y,0) | = | $2$ | Distinct normalized divisorial orders centered at that curve; adding the positive boundary strictly lowers discrepancy. |
| q-small-model-fibre-component-budget | s | ≥ | $b$ | The independent degree vectors of the extended line bundles. |
| q-negative-end-integral-weight-drop | w(2-a^-) | ≥ | $M+1$ | The positive endpoint has an integral scaled deficit, so strict discrepancy improvement crosses a ceiling threshold. |
| q-uniform-local-complement-index | M | = | $M(4,\epsilon,I)$ | The positive integer depends on the displayed data and is independent of the chosen program and endpoint. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-graph-comparison** [claim](#stmt-lem-graph) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-graph): Apply negativity to the graph difference and use relative anti-ampleness to identify its full support. -> Discrepancy monotonicity, exact strictness loci, and bad-locus dimension bounds.
- **step-singularity-preservation** [claim](#stmt-cor-singularities-preserved) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-singularities-preserved): Read singularity conditions from the discrepancy comparison. -> Preservation of the required singularities and strict improvement for lost primes.
- **step-cycle-localization** [claim](#stmt-lem-cycle-rank) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cycle-rank): Use homology localization and positivity of ample intersection with top-dimensional removed cycles. -> Finiteness of divisor loss and the conditional finiteness of negative-side bad surfaces.
- **step-reduce-to-positive-surfaces** [claim](#stmt-cor-surfaces-suffice) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-surfaces-suffice): Combine the cycle-rank reductions with the graph dimension obstruction. -> Termination follows once that surface finiteness is proved.
- **step-identify-nef-and-ample-ends** [claim](#stmt-lem-two-ends) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-two-ends): Apply negativity in both directions, then factor through connected fibres using relative ampleness. -> Equality of pullbacks and a morphism to the prescribed ample end.
- **step-snc-valuation-calculation** [claim](#stmt-lem-snc-valuations) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-snc-valuations): Compare the crepant boundary with the reduced SNC boundary and use a toroidal modification for zero error. -> The discrepancy formula, integral error bound, and bounded-order monomial finiteness.
- **step-finite-low-discrepancy-list** [claim](#stmt-lem-small-discrepancies) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-small-discrepancies): Bound positive SNC orders and separate centers outside the exceptional support. -> A finite list of exceptional valuations with discrepancy at most one.
- **step-classify-ordinary-echoes** [claim](#stmt-lem-echoes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-echoes): Principalize the exceptional label locus and calculate monomial orders on the smooth single-label open. -> Ordinary echo families and a finite exceptional list below two.
- **step-terminal-chain** [claim](#stmt-prop-terminal-chain) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-terminal-chain): Extract the stabilized low-discrepancy list, decrease coefficients, and run finite relative klt bridges whose ends are identified by negativity. -> Crepant terminal junctions with persistent labels and monotone discrepancies.
- **step-transverse-surface-mld** [claim](#stmt-lem-transverse-mld) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transverse-mld): Principalize the center and cut by general ample hyperplanes, preserving the relevant crepant coefficients. -> Attained surface mld values with the same finite boundary coefficient set.
- **step-confine-varying-labels** [claim](#stmt-prop-codim-two) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-codim-two): Apply surface mld ACC to recurring computing divisors and use strict discrepancy improvement at bad centers. -> Smooth generic bad surfaces with mld greater than one and varying labels mapped to curves or points.
- **step-stabilize-defaults** [claim](#stmt-difficulty-preparation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-difficulty-preparation): Use the coefficient bound to make each echo sum finite, then stabilize nonincreasing integer defaults. -> Constant default weights throughout a truncated chain and its finite bridges.
- **step-local-ceiling-budget** [claim](#stmt-lem-ceiling-error) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ceiling-error): Compare normalization branches with multiplicities, apply the ceiling inequality, and pay higher defaults with distinct monomial valuations. -> A local negative allowance controlled only by varying branch excess.
- **step-conductor-estimate** [claim](#stmt-lem-conductor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-conductor): Use normalization excision, degree-weighted cycle pushforward, and strictness of mixed Hodge structures. -> A cohomological bound for excess branches after subtracting divisor-cycle ranks.
- **step-intersection-complex-purity** [claim](#stmt-lem-restriction-purity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-restriction-purity): Apply projective decomposition before restriction and exclude inappropriate supports by stratified stalk bounds. -> Purity of the restricted intersection-complex hypercohomology.
- **step-isolate-singular-curve-local-systems** [claim](#stmt-lem-local-system-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-local-system-bound): Use the constant-to-intersection-complex comparison cone and compactly supported cohomology on curve strata. -> A bound by punctured local-system coinvariant dimensions along singular curves in the boundary union.
- **step-split-punctured-cohomology** [claim](#stmt-local-split-punctured) / [proof](#proof-stmt-local-split-punctured): Use the full-support decomposition summand and its canonical comparison with smooth-open cohomology. -> A split surjection already on the original curve open.
- **step-scheme-fibre-vanishing** [claim](#stmt-local-scheme-fibre-vanishing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-local-scheme-fibre-vanishing): Combine rational singularities, formal functions, and surjective thickening maps; then apply the exponential sequence and projective comparison. -> Coherent cohomology vanishing and spanning Chern classes at closed complex points.
- **step-norm-generic-line-bundles** [claim](#stmt-local-generic-lines) / [proof](#proof-stmt-local-generic-lines): Parameterize line bundles, prove finite monodromy from finite orbits, and take norms to descend invariant classes. -> Independent punctured classes represented by line bundles over the original curve function field.
- **step-extend-bundles-etale** [claim](#stmt-local-etale-lines) / [proof](#proof-stmt-local-etale-lines): Lift through thickenings, algebraize the formal bundles, and approximate in the ordinary henselization. -> Extensions on a pointed ordinary étale neighbourhood with unchanged residue field.
- **step-independent-fibre-degrees** [claim](#stmt-local-degree-independence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-local-degree-independence): Pass to a small factorial model and show that any degree relation descends by relative base point freeness, contradicting punctured independence. -> Independent degree vectors and sufficiently many distinct curve components.
- **step-injective-valuation-restriction** [claim](#stmt-local-restriction-injective) / [proof](#proof-stmt-local-restriction-injective): Base-change a smooth divisorial model and use the residue-field identity to obtain a unique center with unramified uniformizer. -> Injective normalized divisorial restriction preserving discrepancy.
- **step-build-curve-centered-valuations** [claim](#stmt-prop-local-valuations) / [proof](#proof-stmt-local-restriction-injective-2): Blow up generic surface closures of arithmetic fibre curves, restrict their orders injectively, and use positive boundary to lower discrepancy. -> The required distinct curve-centered valuations of the original function field.
- **step-global-branch-budget** [claim](#stmt-thm-branch) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-local-system-bound-2): Combine conductor and local-system estimates, then replace local-system dimensions by distinct valuation counts. -> The global branch inequality.
- **step-contracted-cycle-independence** [claim](#stmt-difficulty-normalization-independence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-difficulty-normalization-independence): Lift a homology relation to a resolution, correct it by exceptional classes, and apply negativity in both signs. -> Linear independence of contracted prime-divisor cycle classes.
- **step-difficulty-monotonicity** [claim](#stmt-prop-difficulty) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-difficulty): Pay local deficits by the branch inequality and cancel changes of branch defaults against exact normalization-rank differences. -> Nonnegative junction values and monotone descent, strict when any exceptional weight drops.
- **step-klt-termination** [claim](#stmt-thm-klt-termination) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-klt-termination): Use integral positive-end discrepancy deficits to force a strict difficulty drop for every positive-side bad surface. -> Termination of every complex klt program in the permitted convention.
- **step-elementary-dlt-existence** [claim](#stmt-lem-dlt-elementary) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-dlt-elementary): Perturb the coefficient-one boundary to klt, construct the elementary operation, and recover the full-boundary sign by relative descent. -> Elementary full-boundary dlt operations preserving the required properties.
- **step-dlt-termination** [claim](#stmt-thm-dlt-termination) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-dlt-termination): Remove divisor loss, apply lower-dimensional special termination, and decrease the boundary away from the surgery to obtain a klt tail. -> Termination of arbitrary elementary dlt fourfold programs.
- **step-find-absolute-vertical-ray** [claim](#stmt-lem-vertical-ray) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-vertical-ray): Minimize on a compact slice of the vertical face and factor the contraction using connected fibres. -> An absolute negative extremal ray with a contraction factoring the structural morphism.
- **step-prescribed-lc-termination** [claim](#stmt-prop-lc-lift) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-lc-lift): Lift the prescribed small tail crepantly, run finite nonempty dlt bridges, and identify their ends with the prescribed positive models. -> An infinite prescribed lc program would produce an impossible infinite elementary dlt program.
- **step-complex-continuation** [claim](#stmt-prop-continuation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-continuation): Add a general effective supplement, obtain a relative good model, and prove its ample model is small by graph support. -> A permitted positive output or the required Mori endpoint.
- **step-numerical-field-invariance** [claim](#stmt-lem-numerical-extension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-numerical-extension): Spread line bundles and integral curves, specialize over an algebraically closed field, and compare intersection degrees. -> Invariance of numerical spaces, curve cones, and relative Picard numbers.
- **step-countable-program-transfer** [claim](#stmt-prop-field-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-field-transfer): Descend all countable diagram and testing data to a common countable algebraically closed field and embed it into the complex numbers. -> A complex program retaining every nontrivial step and chosen ray.
- **step-finite-existence-transfer** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-existence-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-existence-transfer): Spread the finite complex output while keeping the input fixed, then specialize using the constant pullback polarization class. -> The exact contraction and mixed-step output over the original field.
- **step-main-termination-and-endpoints** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-existence-transfer-2): Transfer an infinite prescribed program to the complex case for contradiction and use finite continuation to characterize maximal endpoints. -> Termination for every permitted choice sequence, continuation, and the nef or Mori alternatives.
- **step-semiample-endpoints** [claim](#stmt-cor-semiample-endpoints) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-semiample-endpoints): Transport a nonzero adjoint section through every permitted birational step, exclude a Mori endpoint by intersection, and apply abundance. -> A semiample endpoint for every maximal program in this regime.
- **step-uniform-mori-complements** [claim](#stmt-cor-uniform-mori-complements) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-uniform-mori-complements): Preserve the discrepancy lower bound and coefficient set, exclude nefness by pseudo-effectivity, and apply bounded complements to the Fano type Mori contraction. -> Uniform local klt complements at every resulting Mori endpoint.

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-cycle-rank",
          "exact_quote": "Let $V$ be projective and $A\\subset V$ closed of dimension at most $r$. Restriction to $V\\setminus A$ maps the span of $r$-cycle classes onto the corresponding Borel–Moore cycle span. Its kernel is exactly the span of the classes of the $r$-dimensional irreducible components of $A$. In particular,*\n\n1.  *removing a set of dimension less than $r$ does not change this rank;*\n\n2.  *removing a set with an $r$-dimensional component strictly lowers the rank.*\n\n*For a sequence of diagrams as in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:graph\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:graph\">3</a>, only finitely many steps lose a prime divisor. On a small tail with no positive-side bad surfaces, only finitely many steps have a negative-side bad surface.",
          "source_id": "PM-056.3"
        }
      ],

        "The common open complement of the full bad loci.",

      "output": "Finiteness of divisor loss and the conditional finiteness of negative-side bad surfaces.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-cycle-rank"
    },

If $D_2$ is ample over $Z$, the birational map $V_1\\dashrightarrow V_2$ is a morphism $h$, and $D_1=h^*D_2$.",

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-snc-valuations",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\pi:W\\to X$ be a log resolution of a klt pair, and include the exceptional locus and the crepant boundary support in a simple normal crossings (SNC) divisor $\\sum_jT_j$. Write $a_j=a(T_j;X,B)>0$, assigning $a_j=1$ to any auxiliary component of coefficient zero. If $J$ indexes the components through the generic center of a divisorial valuation $\\xi$ on $W$, then $$\\label{eq:snc-formula}\n a(\\xi;X,B)=\\sum_{j\\in J}\\xi(T_j)a_j+e(\\xi).$$ Here $e(\\xi)$ is a nonnegative integer, the log discrepancy for the reduced SNC boundary near that center. If the center has codimension $c$ in $W$, then $$e(\\xi)\\geq c-|J|.$$ If $e(\\xi)=0$, then $\\xi$ is the monomial valuation associated to a primitive positive integral vector over the generic point of an SNC stratum. In particular there are finitely many such valuations when their orders $\\xi(T_j)$ are bounded.",

        }

In regular parameters $x,t$ at its generic point, with $x=0$ defining that label $S_h$, the contributing valuations are exactly the monomial orders $$\\label{eq:echo-values}\n     \\xi(x)=j,\\quad \\xi(t)=1,\\qquad\n     a(\\xi;Y,\\Theta)=1+j(1-b_h)<2,\\qquad j\\geq1.$$*\n\n*More strongly, all exceptional valuations below two which are not of the form <a href=\"#eq:echo-values\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:echo-values\">[eq:echo-values]</a> on that one-label smooth open belong to a finite list.",

          "exact_quote": "Let $p\\colon Y\\to X$ be a projective birational morphism of normal projective fourfolds over $\\mathbb C$.           "source_id": "PM-056.3"
        }

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-prescribed-program-termination**: Every permitted program from the given projective log canonical fourfold with rational boundary terminates, regardless of the successive permitted choices. Initial factoriality and pseudo-effectivity are unnecessary.
- **invariant-prepared-difficulty-descent**: The corrected difficulty is nonnegative at crepant junctions and nonincreasing across coefficient decreases and small terminal flips. A strict exceptional valuation weight decrease forces a strict difficulty decrease.
- **invariant-branch-excess-budget**: Excess algebraic normalization branches along surfaces are bounded by divisor-cycle ranks on the normalized components and the specified curve-centered valuation count.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-arbitrary-birational-operations**: Apply termination to a sequence augmented by flops or extra blowups. -> The augmented sequence falls outside the permitted program, so its termination is not established.
- **anti-use-uniform-runtime-bound**: Treat the difficulty argument as a uniform computational step budget for all programs from an input. -> Preparation depends on the chosen hypothetical infinite tail, and the source provides no uniform difficulty bound across programs.
- **anti-use-discrepancy-strictness-alone**: Infer a strict ceiling-weight decrease from any strict discrepancy improvement. -> A strict discrepancy increase can remain within the same ceiling interval.
- **anti-use-geometric-sheet-count**: Replace algebraic normalization branches by geometric sheets when evaluating branch excess. -> A divisor of higher residue degree would be overcounted, changing the inequality's left side without the corresponding pushforward accounting.
- **anti-use-residue-field-changing-descent**: Use an arbitrary étale cover to descend the constructed valuations and assume they remain distinct. -> The source's injectivity assertion requires the specified residue-field isomorphism; omitting it removes the justification that distinct valuations remain distinct after restriction.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The principal result quantifies over arbitrary permitted geometric programs and proves termination through a hypothetical infinite tail, auxiliary birational models, and cohomological existence arguments. It supplies no finite input encoding, executable construction of the full program, or effective uniform step budget. The source explicitly states: "The constants $d_h$ may depend on the chosen infinite tail; a bound uniform over different programs is neither asserted nor needed." Its line-bundle argument also invokes a very general point and existence and approximation results. Finite tests of ceiling arithmetic would certify only an auxiliary calculation, not these geometric hypotheses or universal termination.

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Termination concerns geometric programs on projective log canonical fourfolds. The paper supplies no executable representation of arbitrary input pairs or procedure for computing each permitted operation. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The birational operations preserve and improve algebraic singularity properties; they do not define simulation states, physical evolution, or game update rules. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Existence of a contraction and a positive model is a geometric existence result. No finite input encoding, model extraction procedure, or computable stopping test is provided for an engineering tool. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The stated programs terminate independently of successive ray choices, but the proof does not specify effective methods for finding rays, computing flips, or bounding the length of a run. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Cycle-class spans and localization kernels are mathematical invariants of projective varieties. The paper does not construct a finite storage representation or update operations for these spans. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The branch estimate bounds normalization branches using cycle ranks and divisorial valuations. It gives no arithmetic kernel, parallel execution structure, or hardware resource contract. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Injectivity of valuation restriction depends on an étale morphism and a residue-field isomorphism. It does not establish a cryptographic encoding, hardness assumption, or security property. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The difficulty decreases on a prepared terminal chain with specific geometric corrections. No learning model, training objective, or method for evaluating this invariant on learned parameters is constructed. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A negative extremal ray exists in the specified relative geometric setting, but the argument does not provide a finite optimisation formulation or an executable separation procedure for the curve cone. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The terminal chain consists of crepant models joined by coefficient decreases and finite geometric bridges. This supplies no protocol for communication, concurrent updates, or agreement among distributed participants. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The direct application is foundational birational geometry: pseudo-effective inputs have semiample endpoints using log abundance. The paper does not turn this consequence into a finite engineering construction or compute the Cartier multiple. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-main-geometric-regime** (hypothesis): The termination assertion concerns projective lc fourfold pairs with rational boundary over algebraically closed fields of characteristic zero. It does not establish the corresponding assertion outside that regime. Blocked use: ['Apply the theorem to positive characteristic, nonprojective varieties, higher-dimensional pairs, or arbitrary real boundaries.']
- **limit-permitted-transition-relation** (formal_scope): Termination applies to the prescribed negative extremal-ray programs, including mixed steps with a small positive map and the stipulated actual adjoint and boundary transform. Blocked use: ['Insert arbitrary birational modifications and retain the termination guarantee.', 'Replace the negative extremal-ray contraction by an unrestricted face contraction.']
- **limit-excluded-operations** (formal_scope): Flops, extra blowups, and inserted isomorphisms are excluded from the steps counted by the theorem. Blocked use: ['Use the result to rule out an infinite sequence obtained by inserting excluded operations between permitted steps.']
- **limit-positive-relative-picard-number** (formal_scope): The relative Picard-number restriction belongs to the negative contraction. No corresponding condition is imposed on the positive map. Blocked use: ['Reject an otherwise permitted mixed step solely because its positive relative Picard number differs from that of an elementary flip.']
- **limit-full-bad-locus-strictness** (hypothesis): The discrepancy comparison uses the full inverse images of the common base bad locus and the opposite relative ampleness conditions. Strict improvement is confined to valuations whose centers lie in those full bad loci. Blocked use: ['Assert strict discrepancy improvement for every valuation at every step.', 'Replace a full bad locus by only the exceptional locus of its own morphism.']
- **limit-small-tail-cycle-reduction** (hypothesis): The cycle-rank argument removes divisor-losing steps first. Its subsequent control of negative-side bad surfaces requires a small tail without positive-side bad surfaces. Blocked use: ['Apply the negative-side surface finiteness conclusion before establishing the positive-side surface condition.']
- **limit-terminal-chain-preparation** (hypothesis): The difficulty argument requires the stabilized extraction list, persistent labels, the truncation controlling varying-label images, and constant default echo weights throughout the prepared bridges. Blocked use: ['Evaluate an unprepared chain as though its default corrections were constant.', 'Treat a varying extracted coefficient as having a fixed denominator merely because the original boundary is rational.']
- **limit-local-before-global-subtraction** (formal_scope): The difficulty subtracts the ordinary-family defaults separately at each surface before summing. The construction does not authorize subtraction of divergent global valuation sums. Blocked use: ['Compute the raw global valuation total and the raw global default total separately and subtract them without local cancellation.']
- **limit-branch-inequality-geometric-input** (hypothesis): The branch inequality requires the complex projective terminal setting, a rational effective terminal boundary, and a reduced union of positive-coefficient components whose image has dimension at most one. Blocked use: ['Apply the branch estimate to arbitrary boundary unions with higher-dimensional images.', "Use the complex topological estimate directly over another field without the paper's transfer argument."]
- **limit-algebraic-branches-and-cycle-ranks** (formal_scope): Normalization branches are algebraic prime divisors rather than geometric sheets. The accompanying correction uses divisor cycle-class ranks rather than a presumed Picard-number identity on singular varieties. Blocked use: ['Count conjugate geometric sheets as separate algebraic branches.', 'Substitute singular Picard numbers for the normalization cycle ranks without an additional comparison theorem.']
- **limit-actual-scheme-fibres** (formal_scope): The coherent vanishing and line-bundle extension arguments use the actual scheme fibres, including possible nilpotents. Replacing those fibres by their reductions requires separate justification. Blocked use: ['Establish the required coherent vanishing or specified bundle extension only on reduced fibres and infer the scheme-fibre conclusion.']
- **limit-residue-preserving-etale-descent** (hypothesis): The local valuation restriction assertion requires a dominant étale morphism, the specified residue-field isomorphism, and a Q-Gorenstein base. Its injectivity cannot be carried over to an arbitrary residue-field-changing cover. Blocked use: ['Assume distinct constructed valuations remain distinct after restriction while dropping the residue-field identity.']
- **limit-junction-nonnegativity** (edge_case): Nonnegativity is established at crepant junctions. The proof does not require an independent nonnegativity assertion for every intermediate model in a bridge. Blocked use: ['Reject the difficulty construction because an intermediate expression has not separately been shown nonnegative.', 'Use junction nonnegativity as an unconditional statement about arbitrary terminal models.']
- **limit-strictness-needs-integral-end-deficit** (hypothesis): Strict discrepancy improvement alone does not imply a strict ceiling-weight decrease. The positive-side surface argument also requires the integral final scaled deficit. Blocked use: ['Infer a strict difficulty drop from a strict discrepancy increase without checking the denominator-clearing and endpoint-integrality conditions.']
- **limit-no-uniform-program-budget** (nonconstructive): The prepared defaults may depend on the chosen hypothetical infinite tail. The proof does not assert a difficulty bound uniform over different programs or provide an executable complexity contract. Blocked use: ['Advertise a uniform runtime or step budget derived from the prepared difficulty.', 'Treat qualitative finiteness as an implemented method for computing rays, flips, or the invariant.']
- **limit-entire-program-field-transfer** (formal_scope): Termination transfer preserves an entire countable prescribed program, whereas continuation transfers finite diagrams. Existence of a terminating complex program does not substitute for transfer of all the prescribed choices in a hypothetical infinite program. Blocked use: ['Deduce arbitrary termination over the original field solely from existence of a terminating complex program.']
- **limit-semiampleness-additional-input** (hypothesis): The main termination result gives nef or Mori endpoints. The semiample endpoint consequence additionally assumes pseudo-effectivity and invokes log abundance. Blocked use: ['Claim semiampleness solely from termination.', 'Apply the semiample endpoint corollary without its pseudo-effectivity hypothesis.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that every permitted minimal model program for a projective log canonical fourfold with rational boundary over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero terminates. The result allows arbitrary negative extremal rays and mixed birational steps on the given models, without pseudo-effectivity or initial ℚ-factoriality.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction and statement

The minimal model program seeks to simplify a projective variety by operations on which its canonical divisor, or an adjoint divisor $K_X+B$, is negative. A divisorial contraction removes a divisor. A flip replaces a small contraction by a birational model on which the adjoint becomes relatively ample. The expected endpoint is a nef adjoint or a Mori fibre space. Existence of the required operations makes the program possible; termination ensures that a sequence of choices actually reaches an endpoint. It is stronger than the existence of one minimal model or the termination of a particular program with scaling.

The three-dimensional log minimal model program established the basic pattern; see (Kollár 1992; Kollár and Mori 1998) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[KollarEtAl1992](#ref-KollarEtAl1992), [KM98](#ref-KM98)]. In higher dimensions, Birkar–Cascini–Hacon–McKernan proved klt flip existence and minimal-model existence for klt pairs with big boundary and pseudo-effective adjoint (Birkar et al. 2010) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[BCHM](#ref-BCHM)]. Fujino’s cone and contraction theorem and Birkar’s lc flip theorem supply the corresponding operations for log canonical pairs (Fujino 2011; Birkar 2012) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[FujinoFoundations](#ref-FujinoFoundations), [BirkarLC](#ref-BirkarLC)]. In dimension four, Shokurov’s ordered-termination method constructed minimal models or Mori fibre spaces; Birkar gave a short proof of the klt case (Shokurov 2009; Birkar 2009) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[ShokurovOrdered](#ref-ShokurovOrdered), [BirkarShokurov](#ref-BirkarShokurov)]. These constructions use selected programs, and do not establish termination for every successive choice of negative ray.

The difficulty method studies arbitrary sequences by combining discrepancy improvement with the geometry of the exceptional loci. Shokurov introduced discrepancy-counting difficulty (Shokurov 1986, Definition 2.15 and Corollaries 2.16–2.17) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Shokurov1985](#ref-Shokurov1985)]. Kawamata proved termination for terminal fourfolds (Kawamata, Matsuda, and Matsuki 1987) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[KMM87](#ref-KMM87)], and Fujino extended the method to canonical pairs with rational boundary; the weighted difficulty and precise hypotheses are corrected in (Fujino 2004, 2005a) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[FujinoCanonical](#ref-FujinoCanonical), [FujinoCanonicalAddendum](#ref-FujinoCanonicalAddendum)]. Algebraic cycle classes account for the remaining flips of type $(2,1)$ in these arguments. Alexeev–Hacon–Kawamata developed weighted difficulties that subtract the families of valuations arising along a boundary component and include cycle-rank terms on its normalization (Alexeev, Hacon, and Kawamata 2007) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[AHK](#ref-AHK)]. Their Theorem 3.2 proves termination of klt fourfold flips when $-(K_X+B)$ is numerically equivalent to an effective divisor. This numerical effectivity hypothesis does not follow from non-pseudo-effectivity of $K_X+B$.

Arbitrary termination for pseudo-effective lc fourfolds is now available through the generalized-pair theorem of Chen–Tsakanikas (Chen and Tsakanikas 2023, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[ChenTsakanikas](#ref-ChenTsakanikas)] and Moraga’s termination theorem (Moraga 2025, Theorem 1) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Moraga2025](#ref-Moraga2025)]. Both cover ordinary pairs with real boundary and impose no initial $\mathbb Q$-factoriality in their statements about sequences of flips. The unrestricted theorem of Chen–Tsakanikas is three-dimensional. More recently, Han–Liu–Zhuang proved termination of every klt fourfold MMP with big boundary, including the non-pseudo-effective case (Han, Liu, and Zhuang 2025, Corollary 3.2(3)) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[HanLiuZhuang2025](#ref-HanLiuZhuang2025)].

The result below treats ordinary lc fourfolds with rational boundary without a pseudo-effectivity or big-boundary hypothesis. It also includes mixed birational operations on models which need not be $\mathbb Q$-factorial. The proof follows the difficulty approach: its additional ingredient controls the normalization branches that enter the correction terms when coefficients on the auxiliary terminal models continue to decrease.

We prove the projective four-dimensional case of the Termination of Flips Conjecture for ordinary log canonical pairs with rational boundary; see (Chen and Tsakanikas 2023, Introduction) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[ChenTsakanikas](#ref-ChenTsakanikas)] for the conjecture and its generalized-pair formulation. Our program always starts from the given model. We first prove the theorem over $\mathbb C$, where the branch estimate uses topology, and then transfer the full assertion to every algebraically closed characteristic-zero field.

Throughout, a pair $(X,B)$ consists of a normal integral variety and an effective $\mathbb Q$-divisor such that $K_X+B$ is $\mathbb Q$-Cartier. Canonical divisors on birational models are chosen using the same rational top differential. For a normalized divisorial valuation $v=\operatorname{ord}_E$, with $E$ on a smooth model $p:W\to X$, our convention is $$a(v;X,B)=1+\operatorname{coeff}_E\bigl(K_W-p^*(K_X+B)\bigr).$$ Thus $a$ is the *log discrepancy*. Its center $c_X(v)$ is the closed irreducible center. A valuation is exceptional over $X$ when this center has codimension at least two. Log canonical (lc) and klt mean, respectively, that every log discrepancy is nonnegative and positive. A terminal pair is klt and has $a(v;X,B)>1$ for every exceptional valuation. The boundaries in the pair statements are effective; the crepant pullback divisors used on log resolutions may have negative coefficients.

<a id="stmt-def-program"></a>
<div id="def:program" class="definition">

**Definition 1** (Programs and mixed steps). Let $(X,B)$ be a projective lc pair. Set $(X_0,B_0)=(X,B)$ and $D_i=K_{X_i}+B_i$. At a non-nef stage choose any $D_i$-negative extremal ray of $\overline{\operatorname{NE}}(X_i)$ and its projective contraction $$f_i:X_i\longrightarrow Z_i,
 \qquad (f_i)_*\mathcal O_{X_i}=\mathcal O_{Z_i},
 \qquad \rho(X_i/Z_i)=1.$$ The target is normal, and $-D_i$ is relatively ample. Stop if $f_i$ is of fibre type. If it is birational, a permitted step is $$X_i\xrightarrow{\ f_i\ }Z_i
 \xleftarrow{\ f_i^+\ }X_{i+1},$$ where $f_i^+$ is small and projective birational, possibly an isomorphism, $(X_{i+1},B_{i+1})$ is lc, $B_{i+1}$ is the strict transform of $(f_i)_*B_i$, and $D_{i+1}$ is $\mathbb Q$-Cartier and $f_i^+$-ample. There is no condition on $\rho(X_{i+1}/Z_i)$.

This includes divisorial contractions, flips, and a divisorial contraction followed by a small modification. We call the last possibility a *mixed step*. Stop at a nef adjoint as well. Flops, extra blowups, and inserted isomorphisms are not steps. A program is maximal if it is continued whenever neither stopping condition holds. The broader face-contracting convention in (Han, Liu, and Zhuang 2025, Definition 2.5) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[HanLiuZhuang2025](#ref-HanLiuZhuang2025)] also allows such birational diagrams; here the negative contraction is required to contract one extremal ray.

</div>

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 2**. *Let $k$ be an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero, and let $(X,B)$ be a projective lc pair of dimension four with rational boundary. No $\mathbb Q$-factoriality or pseudo-effectivity is assumed.*

1.  *Every negative extremal ray at every stage has the contraction and, when birational, a positive model required by Definition <a href="#def:program" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:program">1</a>.*

2.  *Every program of Definition <a href="#def:program" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:program">1</a> starting from $(X,B)$ has finitely many birational steps, for every sequence of permitted choices.*

3.  *Every maximal program ends either at a projective lc pair with nef adjoint, or at a Mori fibre space: a projective contraction with connected fibres, relative Picard number one, lower-dimensional normal base, and relatively ample negative adjoint.*

*An already nef input has a zero-step completed program.*

</div>

The auxiliary terminal and dlt models used in the proof retain the initial model and every prescribed downstairs choice. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">2</a> is a termination statement; semiampleness of a nef endpoint requires abundance. Section <a href="#sec:endpoints" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:endpoints">10</a> gives that consequence using (OpenAI 2026b) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[OpenAILogAbundance2026](#ref-OpenAILogAbundance2026)].
